## Appendix 

#### Alternative performance measures 

###### **Alternative performance measures** 

An alternative performance measure (an APM) is a financial measure of historical or future financial performance, financial position or cash flows other than a financial measure defined or specified in the applicable recognized accounting standards or in other applicable regulations. A number of APMs are reported in the discussion of the financial and operating performance of the external reports (annual, quarterly and other reports). APMs are used to provide a more complete picture of operating performance and to reflect management’s view of the fundamental drivers of the business results. A definition of each APM, the method used to calculate it and the information content are presented in alphabetical order in the table below. These APMs may qualify as non-GAAP measures as defined by US Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) regulations. 

Credit Suisse‘s assets and liabilities as of 30 June 2023 are reflected in the Group balance sheet measures. Credit Suisse‘s second quarter results for the one-month period ended 30 June 2023, as included in the Group’s second quarter results, have been annualized for the purpose of the calculation of return measures, by multiplying such by four and two for quarterly and semi-annual measures, respectively. 

|**APM label**|**Calculation**|**Information content**|
|---|---|---|
|**Active Digital Banking clients in**<br>**Corporate & Institutional Clients (%)**<br>**– Personal & Corporate Banking**|Calculated as the average number of active clients for<br>each month in the relevant period divided by the<br>average number of total clients. “Clients” refers to the<br>number of unique business relationships or legal<br>entities operated by Corporate & Institutional Clients,<br>excluding clients that do not have an account, mono-<br>product clients and clients that have defaulted on loans<br>or credit facilities. At the end of each month, any client<br>that has logged on at least once in that month is<br>determined to be “active” (a log-in time stamp is<br>allocated to all business relationship numbers or per<br>legal entity in a digital banking contract).|This measure provides information about the<br>proportion of active Digital Banking clients in the total<br>number of UBS clients (within the aforementioned<br>meaning) which are serviced by Corporate &<br>Institutional Clients.|
|**Active Digital Banking clients in**<br>**Personal Banking (%)**<br>**– Personal & Corporate Banking**|Calculated as the average number of active clients for<br>each month in the relevant period divided by the<br>average number of total clients. “Clients” refers to the<br>number of unique business relationships operated by<br>Personal Banking, excluding persons under the age of<br>15, clients who do not have a private account, clients<br>domiciled outside Switzerland and clients who have<br>defaulted on loans or credit facilities. At the end of<br>each month, any client that has logged on at least once<br>in that month is determined to be “active” (a log-in<br>time stamp is allocated to all business relationship<br>numbers in a digital banking contract).|This measure provides information about the<br>proportion of active Digital Banking clients in the total<br>number of UBS clients (within the aforementioned<br>meaning) who are serviced by Personal Banking.|
|**Active Mobile Banking clients in**<br>**Personal Banking (%)**<br>**– Personal & Corporate Banking**|Calculated as the average number of active clients for<br>each month in the relevant period divided by the<br>average number of total clients. “Clients” refers to the<br>number of unique business relationships operated by<br>Personal Banking, excluding persons under the age of<br>15, clients who do not have a private account, clients<br>domiciled outside Switzerland and clients who have<br>defaulted on loans or credit facilities. At the end of<br>each month, any client that has logged on via the<br>mobile app at least once in that month is determined<br>to be “active” (a log-in time stamp is allocated to all<br>business relationship numbers in a digital banking<br>contract).|This measure provides information about the<br>proportion of active Mobile Banking clients in the<br>total number of UBS clients (within the<br>aforementioned meaning) who are serviced by<br>Personal Banking.|



**Second quarter 2023 report |** Appendix 

114 

|**APM label**<br>|**Calculation**<br>|**Information content**<br>|
|---|---|---|
|**Assets under management (USD)**<br>**– Wealth Management (Credit Suisse),**<br>**Swiss Bank (Credit Suisse),**<br>**Asset Management (Credit Suisse)**|Calculated as the sum of assets for which investment<br>advisory or discretionary asset management services<br>are provided, investment fund assets and assets<br>invested in other investment fund-like pooled<br>investment vehicles. In order to be classified as assets<br>under management, a service is expected to be<br>provided currently or in the foreseeable future where<br>the involvement of banking or investment expertise<br>(e.g., as asset manager or investment advisor) is not<br>purely executional or custodial in nature.|This measure provides information about the volume<br>of assets for which investment advisory or<br>discretionary asset management services are provided.|
|**Cost / income ratio (%)**|Calculated as operating expenses divided by total<br>revenues.|This measure provides information about the<br>efficiency of the business by comparing operating<br>expenses with gross income.|
|**Cost / income ratio (excluding**<br>**integration-related expenses and**<br>**acquisition costs) (%)**|Calculated as operating expenses, excluding<br>integration-related expenses and acquisition costs<br>associated with the acquisition of the Credit Suisse<br>Group, divided by total revenues.|This measure provides information about the<br>efficiency of the business by comparing operating<br>expenses, excluding integration-related expenses and<br>acquisition costs associated with the acquisition of the<br>Credit Suisse Group, with gross income.|
|**Cost / income ratio (%)**<br>**– Wealth Management (Credit Suisse),**<br>**Swiss Bank (Credit Suisse),**<br>**Asset Management (Credit Suisse),**<br>**the Investment Bank (Credit Suisse)**|Calculated as total operating expenses divided by net<br>revenues.|This measure provides information about the<br>efficiency of the business by comparing total<br>operating expenses with net revenues.|
|**Fee and trading income for Corporate &**<br>**Institutional Clients (USD and CHF)**<br>**– Personal & Corporate Banking**|Calculated as the total of recurring net fee and<br>transaction-based income for Corporate &<br>Institutional Clients.|This measure provides information about the amount<br>of fee and trading income for Corporate &<br>Institutional Clients.|
|**Fee-generating assets (USD)**<br>**– Global Wealth Management**|Calculated as the sum of discretionary and<br>nondiscretionary wealth management portfolios<br>(mandate volume) and assets where generated<br>revenues are predominantly of a recurring nature, i.e.,<br>mainly investment, mutual, hedge and private-market<br>funds where the firm has a distribution agreement,<br>including client commitments into closed-ended<br>private-market funds from the date that recurring<br>fees are charged. Assets related to the Global<br>Financial Intermediaries business are excluded, as are<br>assets of sanctioned clients.|This measure provides information about the volume<br>of invested assets that create a revenue stream,<br>whether as a result of the nature of the contractual<br>relationship with clients or through the fee structure<br>of the asset. An increase in the level of fee-generating<br>assets results in an increase in the associated revenue<br>stream. Assets of sanctioned clients are excluded from<br>fee-generating assets.|
|**Fee-generating asset margin (bps)**<br>**– Global Wealth Management**|Calculated as revenues from fee-generating assets (a<br>portion of which is included in recurring fee income<br>and a portion of which is included in transaction-<br>based income, annualized as applicable) divided by<br>average fee-generating assets for the relevant<br>mandate fee billing period. For the US, fees have<br>been billed on daily balances since the fourth quarter<br>of 2020 and average fee-generating assets are<br>calculated as the average of the monthly average<br>balances. Prior to the fourth quarter of 2020, billing<br>was based on prior quarter-end balances, and the<br>average fee-generating assets were thus the prior<br>quarter-end balance. For balances outside of the US,<br>billing is based on prior month-end balances and<br>average fee-generating assets are thus the average of<br>the prior month-end balances.|This measure provides information about the revenues<br>from fee-generating assets in relation to their average<br>volume during the relevant mandate fee billing<br>period.|
|**Fee-pool-comparable revenues (USD)**<br>**– the Investment Bank**|Calculated as the total of revenues from: merger-and-<br>acquisition-related transactions; Equity Capital<br>Markets, excluding derivatives; Leveraged Capital<br>Markets, excluding the impact of mark-to-market<br>movements on loan portfolios; and Debt Capital<br>Markets, excluding revenues related to debt<br>underwriting of UBS instruments.|This measure provides information about the amount<br>of revenues in the Investment Bank that are<br>comparable with the relevant global fee pools.|
|**Gross margin on invested assets (bps)**<br>**– Asset Management**|Calculated as total revenues (annualized as applicable)<br>divided by average invested assets.|This measure provides information about the total<br>revenues of the business in relation to invested assets.|
|**Impaired loan portfolio as a percentage**<br>**of total loan portfolio, gross (%)**<br>**– Global Wealth Management,**<br>**Personal & Corporate Banking**|Calculated as impaired loan portfolio divided by total<br>gross loan portfolio.|This measure provides information about the<br>proportion of impaired loan portfolio in the total gross<br>loan portfolio.|
|**Invested assets (USD and CHF)**<br>**– Global Wealth Management,**<br>**Personal & Corporate Banking,**<br>**Asset Management**|Calculated as the sum of managed fund assets,<br>managed institutional assets, discretionary and<br>advisory wealth management portfolios, fiduciary<br>deposits, time deposits, savings accounts, and wealth<br>management securities or brokerage accounts.|This measure provides information about the volume<br>of client assets managed by or deposited with UBS for<br>investment purposes.|



**Second quarter 2023 report |** Appendix 

115 

|**APM label**<br>|**Calculation**<br>|**Information content**<br>|
|---|---|---|
|**Investment products for Personal**<br>**Banking (USD and CHF)**<br>**– Personal & Corporate Banking**|Calculated as the sum of investment funds (including<br>UBS Vitainvest third-pillar pension funds, as well as<br>money market funds), mandates and third-party life<br>insurance operated in Personal Banking.|This measure provides information about the volume<br>of investment funds (including UBS Vitainvest third-<br>pillar pension funds, as well as money market funds),<br>mandates and third-party life insurance operated in<br>Personal Banking.|
|**Net interest margin (bps)**<br>**– Personal & Corporate Banking**|Calculated as net interest income (annualized as<br>applicable) divided by average loans.|This measure provides information about the<br>profitability of the business by calculating the<br>difference between the price charged for lending and<br>the cost of funding, relative to loan value.|
|**Net new assets (USD)**<br>**– Wealth Management (Credit Suisse),**<br>**Swiss Bank (Credit Suisse),**<br>**Asset Management (Credit Suisse)**|Calculated as the net amount of new asset inflows<br>and asset outflows. The calculation is based on the<br>direct method, taking into account individual cash<br>payments, security deliveries and cash flows resulting<br>from loan increases or repayments. Excluded from the<br>calculation are interest and dividend income credited<br>to clients and commissions, interest, and fees charged<br>for banking services, as well as changes in assets<br>under management due to currency and market<br>volatility. Similarly, other effects mainly relate to asset<br>inflows and outflows due to acquisition or divestiture,<br>exit from businesses or markets or exits due to new<br>regulatory requirements are excluded from the<br>calculation.|This measure provides information about the degree<br>of success in acquiring assets under management or<br>changes in assets under management through<br>warranted reclassifications during a specific period.|
|**Net new fee-generating assets (USD)**<br>**– Global Wealth Management**|Calculated as the net amount of fee-generating asset<br>inflows and outflows, including dividend and interest<br>inflows into mandates and outflows from mandate<br>fees paid by clients during a specific period. Excluded<br>from the calculation are the effects on fee-generating<br>assets of strategic decisions by UBS to exit markets or<br>services.|This measure provides information about the<br>development of fee-generating assets during a<br>specific period as a result of net flows, excluding<br>movements due to market performance and foreign<br>exchange translation, as well as the effects on fee-<br>generating assets of strategic decisions by UBS to exit<br>markets or services.|
|**Net new fee-generating asset**<br>**growth rate (%)**<br>**– Global Wealth Management**|Calculated as the net amount of fee-generating asset<br>inflows and outflows recorded during a specific<br>period (annualized as applicable) divided by total fee-<br>generating assets at the beginning of the period.|This measure provides information about the growth<br>of fee-generating assets during a specific period as a<br>result of net new fee-generating asset flows.|
|**Net new investment products for**<br>**Personal Banking (USD and CHF)**<br>**– Personal & Corporate Banking**|Calculated as the net amount of inflows and outflows<br>of investment products during a specific period.|This measure provides information about the<br>development of investment products during a specific<br>period as a result of net new investment product<br>flows.|
|**Net new money (USD)**<br>**– Global Wealth Management,**<br>**Asset Management**|Calculated as the net amount of inflows and outflows<br>of invested assets (as defined in UBS policy) recorded<br>during a specific period. Excluded from the calculation<br>are movements due to market performance, foreign<br>exchange translation, dividends, interest and fees, as<br>well as the effects on invested assets of strategic<br>decisions by UBS to exit markets or services. Net new<br>money is not measured for Personal & Corporate<br>Banking.|This measure provides information about the<br>development of invested assets during a specific<br>period as a result of net new money flows.|
|**Net profit growth (%)**|Calculated as the change in net profit attributable to<br>shareholders from continuing operations between<br>current and comparison periods divided by net profit<br>attributable to shareholders from continuing<br>operations of the comparison period.|This measure provides information about profit<br>growth since the comparison period.|
|**Net profit growth (excluding negative**<br>**goodwill, integration-related expenses,**<br>**and acquisition costs) (%)**|Calculated as the change in net profit attributable to<br>shareholders from continuing operations between<br>current and comparison periods divided by net profit<br>attributable to shareholders from continuing<br>operations of the comparison period. Net profit<br>attributable to shareholders excludes negative<br>goodwill, integration-related expenses and acquisition<br>costs associated with the acquisition of the Credit<br>Suisse Group, and related tax impacts.|This measure provides information about profit<br>growth since the comparison period, while excluding<br>negative goodwill, integration-related expenses and<br>acquisition costs associated with the acquisition of the<br>Credit Suisse Group, and related tax impacts.|
|**Operating profit / (loss) before tax**<br>**(excluding negative goodwill,**<br>**integration-related expenses, and**<br>**acquisition costs) (USD)**|Calculated as total revenues less negative goodwill,<br>less operating expenses, which exclude integration-<br>related expenses and acquisition costs associated with<br>the acquisition of the Credit Suisse Group, less the<br>impact of credit loss expense or release.|This measure provides information about financial<br>performance, excluding negative goodwill,<br>integration-related expenses and acquisition costs<br>associated with the acquisition of the Credit Suisse<br>Group.|
|**Pre-tax profit growth (%)**<br>**– Global Wealth Management,**<br>**Personal & Corporate Banking,**<br>**Asset Management,**<br>**the Investment Bank**|Calculated as the change in net profit before tax<br>attributable to shareholders from continuing<br>operations between current and comparison periods<br>divided by net profit before tax attributable to<br>shareholders from continuing operations of the<br>comparison period.|This measure provides information about pre-tax<br>profit growth since the comparison period.|

|**APM label**|**Calculation**|**Information content**|
|---|---|---|
|**Recurring commissions and fees (USD)**<br>**– Wealth Management (Credit Suisse),**<br>**Swiss Bank (Credit Suisse)**|Calculated as the total of recurring commissions and<br>fees for services, such as investment product<br>management, discretionary mandate and other asset<br>management-related fees, fees from lending<br>activities, fees for general banking products and<br>services and revenues from wealth structuring<br>solutions.|This measure provides information about the amount<br>of recurring commissions and fees.|
|**Recurring net fee income**<br>**(USD and CHF)**<br>**– Global Wealth Management,**<br>**Personal & Corporate Banking**|Calculated as the total of fees for services provided on<br>an ongoing basis, such as portfolio management fees,<br>asset-based investment fund fees and custody fees,<br>which are generated on client assets, and<br>administrative fees for accounts.|This measure provides information about the amount<br>of recurring net fee income.|
|**Return on common equity tier 1**<br>**capital (%)**|Calculated as annualized net profit attributable to<br>shareholders divided by average common equity tier 1<br>capital.|This measure provides information about the<br>profitability of the business in relation to common<br>equity tier 1 capital.|
|**Return on common equity tier 1**<br>**capital (excluding negative goodwill,**<br>**integration-related expenses, and**<br>**acquisition costs) (%)**|Calculated as annualized net profit attributable to<br>shareholders divided by average common equity tier 1<br>capital. Net profit attributable to shareholders and<br>common equity tier 1 capital exclude negative<br>goodwill, integration-related expenses and acquisition<br>costs associated with the acquisition of the Credit<br>Suisse Group, and related tax impacts.|This measure provides information about the<br>profitability of the business in relation to common<br>equity tier 1 capital, while excluding negative<br>goodwill, integration-related expenses and acquisition<br>costs associated with the acquisition of the Credit<br>Suisse Group, and related tax impacts.|
|**Return on equity (%)**|Calculated as annualized net profit attributable to<br>shareholders divided by average equity attributable to<br>shareholders.|This measure provides information about the<br>profitability of the business in relation to equity.|
|**Return on equity (excluding negative**<br>**goodwill, integration-related expenses,**<br>**and acquisition costs) (%)**|Calculated as annualized net profit attributable to<br>shareholders divided by average equity attributable to<br>shareholders. Net profit attributable to shareholders<br>and equity attributable to shareholders exclude<br>negative goodwill, integration-related expenses and<br>acquisition costs associated with the acquisition of the<br>Credit Suisse Group, and related tax impacts.|This measure provides information about the<br>profitability of the business in relation to equity, while<br>excluding negative goodwill, integration-related<br>expenses and acquisition costs associated with the<br>acquisition of the Credit Suisse Group, and related tax<br>impacts.|
|**Return on leverage ratio denominator,**<br>**gross (%)**|Calculated as annualized total revenues divided by<br>average leverage ratio denominator.|This measure provides information about the revenues<br>of the business in relation to the leverage ratio<br>denominator.|
|**Return on tangible equity (%)**|Calculated as annualized net profit attributable to<br>shareholders divided by average equity attributable to<br>shareholders less average goodwill and intangible<br>assets.|This measure provides information about the<br>profitability of the business in relation to tangible<br>equity.|
|**Return on tangible equity (excluding**<br>**negative goodwill, integration-related**<br>**expenses, and acquisition costs) (%)**|Calculated as annualized net profit attributable to<br>shareholders divided by average equity attributable to<br>shareholders less average goodwill and intangible<br>assets. Net profit attributable to shareholders and<br>equity attributable to shareholders exclude negative<br>goodwill, integration-related expenses and acquisition<br>costs associated with the acquisition of the Credit<br>Suisse Group, and related tax impacts.|This measure provides information about the<br>profitability of the business in relation to tangible<br>equity, while excluding negative goodwill, integration-<br>related expenses and acquisition costs associated with<br>the acquisition of the Credit Suisse Group, and related<br>tax impacts.|
|**Tangible book value per share**<br>**(USD)**|Calculated as equity attributable to shareholders less<br>goodwill and intangible assets divided by the number<br>of shares outstanding.|This measure provides information about tangible net<br>assets on a per-share basis.|
|**Total book value per share**<br>**(USD)**|Calculated as equity attributable to shareholders<br>divided bythe number of shares outstanding.|This measure provides information about net assets<br>on aper-share basis.|
|**Total operating expenses (excluding**<br>**integration-related expenses and**<br>**acquisition costs) (USD)**|Calculated as total operating expenses less<br>integration-related expenses and acquisition costs<br>associated with the acquisition of the Credit Suisse<br>Group.|This measure provides information about the amount<br>of total operating expenses excluding integration-<br>related expenses and acquisition costs associated with<br>the acquisition of the Credit Suisse Group.|

#### Information sources 

###### **Reporting publications** 

###### **Annual publications** 

_Annual Report_ : Published in English, this single-volume report provides descriptions of: the Group strategy and performance; the strategy and performance of the business divisions and Group Functions; risk, treasury and capital management; corporate governance, corporate responsibility and the compensation framework, including information about compensation for the Board of Directors and the Group Executive Board members; and financial information, including the financial statements. 

_“Auszug aus dem Geschäftsbericht_ ”: This publication provides a German translation of selected sections of the Annual Report. 

_Compensation Report_ : This report discusses the compensation framework and provides information about compensation for the Board of Directors and the Group Executive Board members. It is available in English and German ( _“Vergütungsbericht_ ”) and represents a component of the Annual Report. 

_Sustainability Report_ : Published in English, the Sustainability Report provides disclosures on environmental, social and governance topics related to the UBS Group. 

_Diversity, Equity and Inclusion Report_ : This report details UBS’s diversity, equity and inclusion priority areas of focus, strategic goals and approach to achieving them. 

###### **Quarterly publications** 

_Quarterly financial report_ : This report provides an update on performance and strategy (where applicable) for the respective quarter. It is available in English. 

The annual and quarterly publications are available in .pdf and online formats at _ubs.com/investors_ , under “Financial information.” Starting with the Annual Report 2022, printed copies, in any language, of the aforementioned annual publications are no longer provided. 

###### **Other information** 

###### **Website** 

The “Investor Relations” website at _ubs.com/investors_ provides the following information about UBS: results-related news releases; financial information, including results-related filings with the US Securities and Exchange Commission (the SEC); information for shareholders, including UBS share price charts, as well as data and dividend information, and for bondholders; the corporate calendar; and presentations by management for investors and financial analysts. Information is available online in English, with some information also available in German. 

###### **Results presentations** 

Quarterly results presentations are webcast live. Recordings of most presentations can be downloaded from _ubs.com/presentations_ . 

###### **Messaging service** 

Email alerts to news about UBS can be subscribed for under “UBS News Alert” at _ubs.com/global/en/investorrelations/contact/investor-services.html_ . Messages are sent in English, German, French or Italian, with an option to select theme preferences for such alerts. 

###### **Form 20-F and other submissions to the US Securities and Exchange Commission** 

UBS files periodic reports with and submits other information to the SEC. Principal among these filings is the annual report on Form 20-F, filed pursuant to the US Securities Exchange Act of 1934. The filing of Form 20-F is structured as a wraparound document. Most sections of the filing can be satisfied by referring to the combined UBS Group AG and UBS AG Annual Report. However, there is a small amount of additional information in Form 20-F that is not presented elsewhere and is particularly targeted at readers in the US. Readers are encouraged to refer to this additional disclosure. Any document that filed with the SEC is available on the SEC’s website: _sec.gov_ . Refer to _ubs.com/investors_ for more information.

**Cautionary Statement Regarding Forward-Looking Statements |** This report contains statements that constitute “forward-looking statements,” including but not limited to management’s outlook for UBS’s financial performance, statements relating to the anticipated effect of transactions and strategic initiatives on UBS’s business and future development and goals or intentions to achieve climate, sustainability and other social objectives. While these forward-looking statements represent UBS’s judgments, expectations and objectives concerning the matters described, a number of risks, uncertainties and other important factors could cause actual developments and results to differ materially from UBS’s expectations. The Russia–Ukraine war continues to affect global markets, exacerbate global inflation, and slow global growth. In addition, the war has caused significant population displacement, and shortages of vital commodities, including energy shortages and food insecurity, and has increased the risk of recession in OECD economies. The coordinated sanctions on Russia and Belarus, and Russian and Belarusian entities and nationals, and the uncertainty as to whether the war will widen and intensify, may continue to have significant adverse effects on the market and macroeconomic conditions, including in ways that cannot be anticipated. UBS’s acquisition of Credit Suisse has materially changed our outlook and strategic direction and introduced new operational challenges. The integration of the Credit Suisse entities into the UBS structure is expected to take between three to five years and presents significant risks, including the risks that UBS Group AG may be unable to achieve the cost reductions and other benefits contemplated by the transaction. This creates significantly greater uncertainty about forward-looking statements. Other factors that may affect our performance and ability to achieve our plans, outlook and other objectives also include, but are not limited to: (i) the degree to which UBS is successful in the execution of its strategic plans, including its cost reduction and efficiency initiatives and its ability to manage its levels of risk-weighted assets (RWA) and leverage ratio denominator (LRD), liquidity coverage ratio and other financial resources, including changes in RWA assets and liabilities arising from higher market volatility and the size of the combined bank; (ii) the degree to which UBS is successful in implementing changes to its businesses to meet changing market, regulatory and other conditions, including as a result of the acquisition of Credit Suisse; (iii) increased inflation and interest rate volatility in major markets; (iv) developments in the macroeconomic climate and in the markets in which UBS operates or to which it is exposed, including movements in securities prices or liquidity, credit spreads, currency exchange rates, deterioration or slow recovery in residential and commercial real estate markets, the effects of economic conditions, including increasing inflationary pressures, market developments, increasing geopolitical tensions, and changes to national trade policies on the financial position or creditworthiness of UBS’s clients and counterparties, as well as on client sentiment and levels of activity, including the COVID-19 pandemic and the measures taken to manage it, which have had and may also continue to have a significant adverse effect on global and regional economic activity, including disruptions to global supply chains and labor market displacements; (v) changes in the availability of capital and funding, including any adverse changes in UBS’s credit spreads and credit ratings of UBS, Credit Suisse, sovereign issuers, structured credit products or credit-related exposures, as well as availability and cost of funding to meet requirements for debt eligible for total loss-absorbing capacity (TLAC), in particular in light of the acquisition of Credit Suisse; (vi) changes in central bank policies or the implementation of financial legislation and regulation in Switzerland, the US, the UK, the European Union and other financial centers that have imposed, or resulted in, or may do so in the future, more stringent or entity-specific capital, TLAC, leverage ratio, net stable funding ratio, liquidity and funding requirements, heightened operational resilience requirements, incremental tax requirements, additional levies, limitations on permitted activities, constraints on remuneration, constraints on transfers of capital and liquidity and sharing of operational costs across the Group or other measures, and the effect these will or would have on UBS’s business activities; (vii) UBS’s ability to successfully implement resolvability and related regulatory requirements and the potential need to make further changes to the legal structure or booking model of UBS in response to legal and regulatory requirements and any additional requirements due to its acquisition of Credit Suisse, or other developments; (viii) UBS’s ability to maintain and improve its systems and controls for complying with sanctions in a timely manner and for the detection and prevention of money laundering to meet evolving regulatory requirements and expectations, in particular in current geopolitical turmoil; (ix) the uncertainty arising from domestic stresses in certain major economies; (x) changes in UBS’s competitive position, including whether differences in regulatory capital and other requirements among the major financial centers adversely affect UBS’s ability to compete in certain lines of business; (xi) changes in the standards of conduct applicable to our businesses that may result from new regulations or new enforcement of existing standards, including measures to impose new and enhanced duties when interacting with customers and in the execution and handling of customer transactions; (xii) the liability to which UBS may be exposed, or possible constraints or sanctions that regulatory authorities might impose on UBS, due to litigation, contractual claims and regulatory investigations, including the potential for disqualification from certain businesses, potentially large fines or monetary penalties, or the loss of licenses or privileges as a result of regulatory or other governmental sanctions, as well as the effect that litigation, regulatory and similar matters have on the operational risk component of our RWA, including as a result of its acquisition of Credit Suisse, as well as the amount of capital available for return to shareholders; (xiii) the effects on UBS’s business, in particular cross-border banking, of sanctions, tax or regulatory developments and of possible changes in UBS’s policies and practices; (xiv) UBS’s ability to retain and attract the employees necessary to generate revenues and to manage, support and control its businesses, which may be affected by competitive factors; (xv) changes in accounting or tax standards or policies, and determinations or interpretations affecting the recognition of gain or loss, the valuation of goodwill, the recognition of deferred tax assets and other matters; (xvi) UBS’s ability to implement new technologies and business methods, including digital services and technologies, and ability to successfully compete with both existing and new financial service providers, some of which may not be regulated to the same extent; (xvii) limitations on the effectiveness of UBS’s internal processes for risk management, risk control, measurement and modeling, and of financial models generally; (xviii) the occurrence of operational failures, such as fraud, misconduct, unauthorized trading, financial crime, cyberattacks, data leakage and systems failures, the risk of which is increased with cyberattack threats from both nation states and non-nation-state actors targeting financial institutions; (xix) restrictions on the ability of UBS Group AG to make payments or distributions, including due to restrictions on the ability of its subsidiaries to make loans or distributions, directly or indirectly, or, in the case of financial difficulties, due to the exercise by FINMA or the regulators of UBS’s operations in other countries of their broad statutory powers in relation to protective measures, restructuring and liquidation proceedings; (xx) the degree to which changes in regulation, capital or legal structure, financial results or other factors may affect UBS’s ability to maintain its stated capital return objective; (xxi) uncertainty over the scope of actions that may be required by UBS, governments and others for UBS to achieve goals relating to climate, environmental and social matters, as well as the evolving nature of underlying science and industry and the possibility of conflict between different governmental standards and regulatory regimes; (xxii) the ability of UBS to access capital markets; (xxiii) the ability of UBS to successfully recover from a disaster or other business continuity problem due to a hurricane, flood, earthquake, terrorist attack, war, conflict (e.g., the Russia–Ukraine war), pandemic, security breach, cyberattack, power loss, telecommunications failure or other natural or man-made event, including the ability to function remotely during long-term disruptions such as the COVID-19 (coronavirus) pandemic; (xxiv) the level of success in the absorption of Credit Suisse, in the integration of the two groups and their businesses, and in the execution of the planned strategy regarding cost reduction and divestment of any non-core assets, the existing assets and liabilities currently existing in the Credit Suisse Group, the level of resulting impairments and write-downs, the effect of the consummation of the integration on the operational results, share price and credit rating of UBS – delays, difficulties, or failure in closing the transaction may cause market disruption and challenges for UBS to maintain business, contractual and operational relationships; and (xxv) the effect that these or other factors or unanticipated events, including media reports and speculations, may have on our reputation and the additional consequences that this may have on our business and performance. The sequence in which the factors above are presented is not indicative of their likelihood of occurrence or the potential magnitude of their consequences. Our business and financial performance could be affected by other factors identified in our past and future filings and reports, including those filed with the US Securities and Exchange Commission (the SEC). More detailed information about those factors is set forth in documents furnished by UBS and filings made by UBS with the SEC, including the Annual Report on Form 20-F for the year ended 31 December 2022. UBS is not under any obligation to (and expressly disclaims any obligation to) update or alter its forward-looking statements, whether as a result of new information, future events, or otherwise. 

**Rounding |** Numbers presented throughout this report may not add up precisely to the totals provided in the tables and text. Percentages and percent changes disclosed in text and tables are calculated on the basis of unrounded figures. Absolute changes between reporting periods disclosed in the text, which can be derived from numbers presented in related tables, are calculated on a rounded basis. 

**Tables |** Within tables, blank fields generally indicate non-applicability or that presentation of any content would not be meaningful, or that information is not available as of the relevant date or for the relevant period. Zero values generally indicate that the respective figure is zero on an actual or rounded basis. Values that are zero on a rounded basis can be either negative or positive on an actual basis.
